## Finite Types and Their Embeddings

Th<sub>e ver</sub>t<sub>ex</sub> t<sub>ypes correspon</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>co</sub>l<sub>ors o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> 1-WL under set a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation (Weisfeiler and Leman 1968; Cai, Fürer, and Immerman 1992; Grohe 2017). Recall that ATP is the fixed set of atomic types.

Definition 1 (Types). The sequence $\langle \mathsf { T P } ^ { ( \ell ) } \rangle _ { \ell \geq 0 }$ of types is: ${ \mathsf { T P } } ^ { ( 0 ) } = { \mathsf { A T P } }$ <sub>, an</sub>d $\mathsf { T P } ^ { ( \ell + 1 ) } = \{ ( t , S ) \ | \ t \in \mathsf { T P } ^ { ( \bar { \ell } ) } , S \subseteq \mathsf { T P } ^ { ( \ell ) } \}$ The atomic type of type t is a $\mathsf { t } \mathsf { p } ( \alpha ) = \alpha f o r \alpha \in \mathsf { A T P }$ <sub>, an</sub>d $\mathsf { a t p } ( t ) = \mathsf { a t p } ( t ^ { \prime } ) f o r t = ( t ^ { \prime } , S ^ { \prime } ) \in \mathsf { T P } ^ { ( \ell ) } w i t h \ell \geq 1$

The (finite) type $\tau ^ { ( \ell ) } ( x , u ) j$ for pointed graph $( x , u )$ <sub>an</sub>d $\ell \geq$ 0 is given by $\tau ^ { ( 0 ) } ( x , u ) = \mathsf { a t p } ( u )$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $\tau ^ { ( \ell + 1 ) } ( x , u ) = ( t , S )$ $i f f \tau ^ { ( \ell ) } ( x , u ) = t$ <sub>an</sub>d $S = \{ \tau ^ { ( \ell ) } ( x , v ) \mid E ( u , v ) \}$

The t<sub>y</sub>pes at la<sub>y</sub>er ℓ correspond to the equivalence classes of an ℓ-bisimulation relation under set-based aggregation: t<sub>wo ver</sub>ti<sub>ces o</sub>f th<sub>e same</sub> t<sub>ype o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e same em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> <sub>regar</sub>dl<sub>ess o</sub>f th<sub>e arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> H<sub>ence,</sub> th<sub>e em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>-</sub>b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ne</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>se</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>aggrega</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>are</sub> fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e</sub> <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ypes:</sub>

Definition 2. The embedding function $\mathbb { E } _ { t } : \Theta \to \mathbb { R } ^ { W }$ , one per type t, maps parameters into embeddings:

1. for $\alpha \in \mathsf { A T P } , \mathbb { E } _ { \alpha } ( \theta ) = e _ { \alpha }$ is a constantfunction ofθ, and 2. for (t, S) in TP<sup>(ℓ+1)</sup> where t is of atomic type $\alpha ,$ $\mathbb { E } _ { ( t , S ) } ( \theta ) = \operatorname { N e t } _ { \ell } ( \alpha , \mathbb { E } _ { t } ( \theta ) , \{ \mathbb { E } _ { t ^ { \prime } } ( \theta ) \mid t ^ { \prime } \in S \} )$

When θ is clearfrom context, we write $\mathbb { E } _ { t }$ instead of $\mathbb { E } _ { t } ( \theta )$ Theorem 17. Let θ be a parameter, let $( x , u )$ b<sub>e a po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $\ell \geq 0$ b<sub>e a</sub> l<sub>ayer</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> Th<sub>en,</sub> $u _ { x , \theta } ^ { ( \ell ) } = \mathbb { E } _ { t } ( \theta ) f o r t = \tau ^ { ( \ell ) } ( x , u ) .$

Definition 4. For each type t, there is formula $\tau _ { t } ( z )$ th<sub>a</sub>t holds in pointed graph (x, u) $i f f \tau ^ { ( \ell ) } ( x , u ) = t$ where ℓ is the layer oft:

Per definition, vertex u satisfies $\tau _ { ( t , S ) }$ if it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ype</sub> i<sub>s</sub> $t ,$ <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>ne</sub>i<sub>g</sub>hb<sub>or</sub> h<sub>as a</sub> t<sub>ype</sub> i<sub>n</sub> $S ,$ <sub>, an</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h t<sub>ype</sub> i<sub>n</sub> $S$ i<sub>s w</sub>it<sub>nesse</sub>d b<sub>y some ne</sub>i<sub>g</sub>hb<sub>or.</sub> A l<sub>og</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ows w</sub>h<sub>ose soun</sub>d<sub>ness</sub> i<sub>s es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on on</sub> $\ell :$

Definition 5. Let θ be a parameter for the architecture $( W , L , \mathsf { A T P } )$ of width W, depth $L ,$ and atomic types in ATP. The logical characterization of the embeddings at layer ℓ is the vector

$$
\Psi ^ { ( \ell ) } ( z ) = \langle \psi _ { 1 } ^ { ( \ell ) } ( z ) , \dots , \psi _ { W } ^ { ( \ell ) } ( z ) \rangle
$$

## Appendix for section: Recurrent Nets

Lemma 17 (Bisimulation invariance). Let $( x , u )$ <sub>an</sub>d $( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } )$ be two pointed (finite) graphs. Then, $( x , u )$ <sub>an</sub>d $( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } )$ are bi<sub>s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar,</sub> <sub>wr</sub>itt<sub>en</sub> <sub>as</sub> $( x , u ) \sim ( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } ) , i f f \tau ( x , u ) \stackrel { } { = } \tau ( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } )$ <sub>.</sub> H<sub>ence,</sub> $i f ( x , u ) \sim ( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } )$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>en</sub> $\Phi ( \tau ( x , u ) ) = \Phi ( \tau ( x ^ { \prime } , u ^ { \prime } ) )$

Theorem 17 (Monotone nets). Let $\operatorname { N e t } ( \cdot , \cdot , \cdot )$ be a converging net over the atomic types in ATP. Step 2b: the game’s run is ≤-non-decreasing. We show by induction on k, $E _ { u } ^ { k } \ \le \ E _ { u } ^ { k + 1 }$ for every vertex u. Base case: $\tilde { E _ { u } ^ { 0 } } \bar { = } F _ { \alpha } ( \emptyset ) \stackrel { \smile } { \le } F _ { \alpha } ( S _ { u } ^ { 0 } ) = E _ { u } ^ { 1 }$ b<sub>y</sub> L<sub>emma</sub> A<sub>, s</sub>i<sub>nce</sub> $\oplus \varnothing = 0 \stackrel { \cdot } { \le } \oplus S _ { u } ^ { 0 }$ <sub>.</sub> I<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>ve s</sub>t<sub>ep:</sub> if $E _ { \circ } ^ { k - 1 } \leq E _ { \circ } ^ { k }$ for every vertex v, then $\oplus \tilde { S } _ { u } ^ { k - 1 } \le \mathrm { \tilde { \oplus } } \bar { S } _ { u } ^ { k }$ b<sub>y</sub> the monotonicit<sub>y</sub> of the a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ator (both sets indexed b<sub>y</sub> $N ( u ) )$ ). Step 2c: admissibility. Two bounds sandwich the run of the game.

## Appendix for section: Equivalence with BΣ<sup>⋄</sup><sub>1</sub>

Theorem 17 (Net-to-logic). Let $\operatorname { N e t } ( \cdot , \cdot , \cdot )$ b<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub> R<sub>atchet</sub> <sub>ne</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>voca</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ary</sub> $\mathcal { V } = \{ e _ { 1 } , \ldots , e _ { m } \}$ of size m. There are BΣ<sup>⋄</sup> formulas $\gamma _ { 1 } , \ldots , \gamma _ { m }$ such that for any pointed graph $( x , u )$ <sub>an</sub>d $i \in \{ 1 , \ldots , m \} : \Phi ( \tau ( \dot { x } , u ) ) = e _ { i } i f f ( x , u ) \models \gamma _ { i } .$

Proof. Let x be a finite graph. Define the subsets $T _ { i } ~ = ~ \{ u ~ \in ~ V ( x ) ~ \mid ~ \Phi ( \tau ( x , u ) ) ~ \succeq ~ e _ { i } \}$ f<sub>or</sub> $i = 1 , \ldots , m$ <sub>.</sub> F<sub>or su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $X _ { 1 } , \ldots , X _ { m } \subseteq V ( x )$ <sub>,</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> $\mathbf { \bar { \Theta } } { \cdot \theta _ { i } } ( v ; X _ { 1 } , \ldots , X _ { m } )$ as

$$
\bigvee _ { ( \alpha , S ) : { \mathscr T } ( \alpha , S ) \succeq e _ { i } } \left( \alpha ( v ) \wedge \bigwedge _ { j \in S } \exists w { \big ( } E ( v , w ) \wedge X _ { j } ( w ) { \big ) } \right) .

These are guarded formulas jointly positive in $X _ { i }$ b<sub>y</sub> construction (no ne<sub>g</sub>ation a<sub>pp</sub>ears). Clusters. For each block $\beta ,$ <sub>cons</sub>id<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> $\beta ,$ <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> $X  Y$ if Y appears within th<sub>e scope o</sub>f $X$ <sub>, an</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> $Y  X$ if X appears as a free variable in the body for Y. A cluster C is a strongly connected component in this graph, and the variable X is the head of C if every other variable Y in C is within the scope of X.

Atomic types. As discussed above, we take the propositions in γ to be the atomic types of the graphs; α ⊨ p abbreviates that type α makes p true. When the propositions are not disjoint or exhaustive, we shall consider their jointly Boolean valuations as th<sub>e se</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>t<sub>om</sub>i<sub>c</sub> t<sub>ypes.</sub>

Lemma H (Fixed points of the block map). Fix a graph x and a block $\beta$ <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>er se</sub>t $R _ { \beta }$ <sub>, an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $f$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e sync</sub>h<sub>ronous</sub> one-step map of the net restricted to $R _ { \beta }$ , a self-map of $\{ 0 , 1 \} ^ { R _ { \beta } \times V ( x ) }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>en,</sub>

(a) f is monotone;

<sup>C</sup>onse<sub>q</sub>uent<sup>l</sup><sub>y</sub>, $\operatorname { l f p } ( f ) = z ^ { \mathrm { s e m } }$ on $R _ { \beta }$

## Examples

Formula $\gamma = \mu X . ( \mathbf { r } \lor \diamond X ) \land \lnot \mu Y . ( \mathbf { g } \lor \diamond Y )$

γ is the classic exam<sub>p</sub>le for reachabilit<sub>y</sub>–safet<sub>y</sub> Boolean combinations (reach the red avoid the <sub>g</sub>reen). The lo<sub>g</sub>ic-to-net translator <sub>g</sub>ives a net of width 2 (after colla<sub>p</sub>sin<sub>g</sub> redundant dimensions that track the two fixed <sub>p</sub>oint com<sub>p</sub>utations for reachabilit<sub>y</sub> and safety). A further application of the net-to-logic recovers γ.

## Methodology

For a closed BΣ<sup>⋄</sup> formula γ over propositions $P \colon$

1. logic-to-net. Compile γ into a concrete net ${ \mathrm { N e t } } = ( A , B , C , \rho { = } { \mathrm { c l i p } } _ { [ 0 , 1 ] } )$ t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith i<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> $e _ { \alpha } ,$ one per joint B<sub>oo</sub>l<sub>ean va</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $\alpha \in 2 ^ { P }$ , followin<sub>g</sub> the re<sub>g</sub>ister table of the <sub>p</sub>roof of Theorem 17 verbatim (one re<sub>g</sub>ister <sub>p</sub>er subformula, one latch <sub>p</sub>er bound variable; no re<sub>g</sub>ister minimization).

2. Extraction. Recover a finite vocabulary V and quotient table $\tau$ : atoms $\times ~ 2 ^ { \nu }  \nu$ from Net by simulating the frozen s<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub><sup>l</sup>e-vertex s<sub>y</sub>stem $z _ { \ell + 1 } = \rho ( A z _ { \ell } + B m + C )$ to a fixed point for every atom and every aggregate value m reachable by com onentwise max from alread -discovered vocabular elements (mirrorin Ste 2 of the roof of Theorem 17). The order $\preceq$ is the com<sub>p</sub>onentwise order restricted to core re<sub>g</sub>isters onl<sub>y</sub> (Ste<sub>p</sub> 3 of that <sub>p</sub>roof): re<sub>g</sub>isters realizin<sub>g</sub> a <sub>g</sub>eneric ne<sub>g</sub>ation (re-attachin<sub>g</sub> a dualized block’s <sub>p</sub>olarit<sub>y</sub>) are antitone and are excluded, exactl<sub>y</sub> as the <sub>p</sub>roof’s construction requires.

3. net-to-logic. Compile T into the guarded, positive equation system $\{ \zeta _ { i } \} _ { i = 1 } ^ { m } .$ (one equation <sub>p</sub>er vocabular<sub>y</sub> element), without <sub>per</sub>f<sub>orm</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> B<sub>e</sub>kič <sub>e</sub>li<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t fl<sub>a</sub>tt<sub>ens</sub> th<sub>e sys</sub>t<sub>em</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>as</sub> $\theta _ { i } , \gamma _ { i }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e remar</sub>k <sub>a</sub>b<sub>ou</sub>t B<sub>e</sub>kič<sub>-</sub>fl<sub>a</sub>tt<sub>ene</sub>d f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>as</sub> <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e</sub> <sub>exponen</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> l<sub>arger</sub> th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>em</sub> th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>come</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>sur</sub>f<sub>aces.</sub> B<sub>u</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>nce</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>nee</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> recom<sub>p</sub>iled quer<sub>y</sub>, not dis<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> it, we evaluate the s<sub>y</sub>stem directl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>y</sub> a <sub>g</sub>lobal (simultaneous) Kleene iteration – exactl<sub>y</sub> the <sub>prac</sub>ti<sub>ce</sub> i<sub>n ver</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on – ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> i<sub>ncur</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bl<sub>ow-up o</sub>f fl<sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

4. Recovery and comparison. For vertex $u , \Phi ( \tau ( x , u ) ) = i \mathrm { i f f } u \vdash \Psi _ { i }$ <sub>an</sub>d<sub>,</sub> f<sub>or every</sub> $j$ <sub>w</sub>ith $i \prec j$ cover<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> $i , u \not \in \Psi _ { j } ,$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> characterization <sub>p</sub>rovided b<sub>y</sub> the theorem, evaluated directl<sub>y</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ainst the s<sub>y</sub>stem’s fixed <sub>p</sub>oint (no Bekič needed for this either). reach\_or\_invariant\_avoid and combined\_three\_way involve three independent propositions, hence 8 atomic types instead of 2–4; extraction produces a vocabulary of 64 elements for both (versus at most 16 for every two-proposition <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ert<sub>y</sub>), and the resultin<sub>g g</sub>uarded equations are corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub>l<sub>y</sub> lar<sub>g</sub>e. Our net-to-lo<sub>g</sub>ic evaluator is a <sub>p</sub>lain <sub>g</sub>lobal Kleene iteration with no cross-round memoization, so its cost scales with (rounds ≈ $| \nu | \cdot n ) \times$ (total equation size) × (n, for the set o<sub>p</sub>erations). For these two <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>erties this makes the main swee<sub>p</sub>’s $n \in \{ 6 , 1 \dot { 0 } , \dot { 1 } 4 , 2 0 , 2 5 \}$ , 900-check scale impractical to <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> ti<sub>me ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> R<sub>ows</sub> 7 <sub>an</sub>d 9 <sub>o</sub>f T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 1 i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t <sub>a sma</sub>ll<sub>er, s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-s</sub>i<sub>ze sweep</sub> $( n = 5 ,$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> th<sub>ree e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> probabilities, two graphs per probability: 6 graphs, 30 checks each).
